## Document 012: 158円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 ～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の_三つの分岐点_が方向性を決める～

Source PDF: all report pdf/158円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 ～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の_三つの分岐点_が方向性を決める～.pdf

Investment Navigator

2026 年 10 月 22 日

1/6 
158 円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 
～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の"三つの分岐点"が方向性を決める～ 
 経済調査部 シニア・フェロー  嶌峰 義清 
（℡：050-5474-7489）

（要旨） 
○ ドル円相場は、7 月末の日米協調介入、9 月上旬の急激な円高、9 月の日米同時利上げを経て、
10 月に入ってからは1 ドル＝158 円前後の狭いレンジでの推移が続いている。円先物市場に
おける非商業部門の建玉は、9 月前半に過去に例のない規模で円の売り越しから買い越しへ
と転じた。しかし、その後は買い越し幅が縮小し、売建玉が再び増え始めている。投機筋の
円買い意欲は弱まりつつあると言えよう。 
○ 期待インフレ率 （BEI） で実質化した日米の実質金利差は、政策金利・2 年債・10 年債のいず
れでも 2％ポイントを超える米国優位が続いている。名目金利差は縮小したものの、米国の
金利上昇は実質金利の上昇、日本のは期待インフレ率の上昇によるところが大きい。このた
め、実質ベースのドル優位は崩れていない。 
○ 当面の焦点は、①11 月 3 日の米中間選挙、②選挙後のイラン情勢、③日銀の 12 月利上げと
その後のペース、の三つである。民主党が上下両院を制し、イラン情勢が沈静化に向かい、
日銀が利上げ路線を明確にすれば、円高方向への転換が視野に入る。逆の組み合わせとなれ
ば、再び160 円台を試し、介入への警戒が強まる展開も否定できない。

1. 投機筋の円ポジションは"円売り一色"から変化し、158 円を挟んで膠着が続くドル円相場 
ドル円相場は、方向感を欠いた推移が続いている。10 月入り後の値動きは、高値が158.94 円
（10 月14 日） 、安値が156.99 円（10 月2 日）と、月間の値幅は2 円弱にとどまる。 
もっとも、ここに至るまでの道のりは平坦ではなかった。4 月末から5 月の大型連休中には、
160 円台後半まで進んだ円安に対して、政府・日銀が総額11.7 兆円の円買い介入を実施した。そ
れでも円安基調は止まらず、7 月 28 日には163.91 円（ECB参照値）と、約40 年ぶりの円安水準を
つけた。これに対し、7 月 31 日には日米当局が28 年ぶりとなる円買い協調介入に踏み切った。介
入額は 15 兆3993 億円に達し、年初からの累計は27 兆 1342 億円と暦年で過去最大を更新した。 
その後は円高へと自律的な反発も見られた。9 月2～3 日には、日銀の追加利上げ観測の強まり
や米雇用関連指標の下振れを受けてドル円は1 日で3 円以上下落、9 日には153 円台まで円高が進
んだ。この局面では介入実績は無く、ジャクソンホール会議でのウォーシュFRB議長の発言をきっ

### Page 2

2/6 
かけに積み上がったドル買い・円売りの持ち高が、損切りを迫られた結果と考えられる。 
ところが、その後は再び円安に押し戻された。FRBは 9 月 16 日に約3 年ぶりの利上げに踏み切
り、政策金利を3.75～4.00％とした。日銀も18 日に政策金利を1.25％へ引き上げたものの、日
米がそろって利上げしたことで金利差は大きく変わらず、ドル円は158 円台へと戻っている。 
為替市場における"投機的な動き"の指標とされるのが、円先物市場における非商業部門（主に
ヘッジファンドなどとされる）の建玉である。その推移を見ると、ここ数ヶ月で大きな変化が生
じていることがわかる（図表1）。 
6 月 2 日時点の円売建玉は24 万 4416 枚と、円キャリートレードがブームだった2007 年以来の
高水準に達していた。差引（ネット）でも12 万9567 枚の売り越しだった。その後も、9 月1 日時
点では 9 万2227 枚の売り越しと、円売り超の状態が続いていた。 
しかし、9 月前半の急激な円高を境に状況は一変し、9 月 15 日時点では差引が12 万 0359 枚の
買い越しへと転じ、わずか2 週間で円買い方向に21 万枚強も振れた。この振れ幅は、統計をさか
のぼれる1986 年以降で最大である。同時点の円買建玉（23 万 7951 枚）も過去最大を記録した。 
もっとも、その後は買い越し幅の縮小が続いている。10 月13 日時点では、買建玉19 万 1256 枚
に対し売建玉14 万 1049 枚で、差引の買い越しは5 万0207 枚とピークの半分以下に縮んだ。売建
玉は 9 月末の12 万枚台から14 万枚台へと再び増えており、円売りの持ち高が積み上がり始めて
いる兆しもうかがえる。投資主体別に見ると、レバレッジド・ファンドは10 月 13 日時点で3 万
3673 枚の円売り越しとなっており、ヘッジファンドの円買い意欲がすでに後退していることを示
している。 
足元の売建玉は、過去の介入局面のように当局が介入を急がなければならない水準にはない。
一方で、9 月に積み上がった円の買い持ちの解消そのものが、円安圧力として作用している面もあ
ると考えられる。

2.  実質金利差から見たドル円相場
為替相場の方向性を最終的に決めるのは、日米の金融政策と、それを反映した金利差である。
そこで、日米の実質金利差を、政策金利・2 年債・10 年債の3 つの年限で見てみる（図表2） 。こ
こでは、実質化に用いる物価として、市場が織り込む期待インフレ率であるBEI（ブレーク・イー
ブン・インフレ率、10 年債）を用いた。足元の消費者物価は、米国では原油高で押し上げられ、
日本では電気・ガス料金支援で押し下げられており、実勢を見誤りやすいためである。
名目で見ると、政策金利は米FF金利の誘導目標が3.75～4.00％、日本の無担保コール翌日物が
1.25％である。10 月 21 日時点の国債利回りは、米国が2 年債4.79％・10 年債 5.13％、日本が2
年債 1.886％・10 年債2.882％となっている。一方、10 年BEIは米国が2.35％（10 月20 日） 、日
本が 2.1～2.2％程度と、ほぼ同水準である。これを差し引いて実質化すると、筆者の試算ではお

おむね次のようになる。
✓ 政策金利：米国＋1.5～1.6％程度、日本▲0.9～1.0％程度（米国優位・差は約2.5％ポイント）
✓ 2 年債：米国＋2.4％程度、日本▲0.3％程度（米国優位・差は約2.7％ポイント）
✓ 10 年債：米国＋2.8％程度、日本＋0.7％程度（米国優位・差は約2.1％ポイント）
名目で見れば、日本の10 年債利回りが9 月下旬に3.115％と約 30 年ぶりの水準に達するなど、
日米金利差は縮小してきた。しかし、BEIで実質化すると景色は異なる。すべての年限で米国の実
質金利が2％ポイント以上も上回っており、ドル優位の構図はほとんど崩れていないのである。
その背景には、日米で金利上昇の"中身"が異なることがある。米国では、昨年末から長期金利
が約 1％ポイント上昇したが、BEIは 2.25％から2.35％へとほぼ横ばいにとどまった。名目金利の
注：BEIで実質化

4/6 
上昇は、ほぼそのまま実質金利の上昇となり、10 年物の実質利回りは9 月末に 2.93％と2008 年
以来の高水準に達した。これに対し日本では、BEIが過去最高圏の2％台まで上昇しており、名目
金利の上昇の相応の部分を期待インフレ率の上昇が占めている。実際、9 月の日銀の利上げ後も
BEIはむしろ上昇した。日本の金利上昇は"インフレ期待による金利上昇"の色彩が濃く、実質金利
の上昇は限られていると言えよう。 
日本の政策金利は、実質では依然として▲1％近いマイナス圏にあり、金融環境はなお緩和的
だ。日銀の利上げや介入にもかかわらず円安の流れが変わらなかったのは、こうした実質金利差
の構図で説明できる面が大きい。逆に言えば、日本の期待インフレ率が2％近辺で落ち着いたまま
利上げが進むか、米国の実質金利が低下に転じない限り、本格的な円高への転換は難しいと考え
られる。 
なお、日本の物価連動国債は発行残高が約10 兆円と小さく、BEIには流動性の影響も含まれ
る。政策金利や2 年債を10 年BEIで実質化するのも簡便的な手法である点には留意が必要だ。

3.  中間選挙の結果次第で変わるドルの行方 
11 月3 日には米国の中間選挙が行われる。1934 年以降の23 回の中間選挙のうち、現職大統領
の政党が上下両院とも議席を増やしたのはわずか2 回しかない。今回も、ロイター通信の世論調
査ではトランプ大統領の支持率が過去最低の31％まで落ち込み、投票先では民主党47％、共和党
35％と 12 ポイントの差がついている。選挙予測サイトのディシジョン・デスクHQは、民主党が下
院を制する確率を78％、上院を制する確率を56％と見ている。 
結果ごとに、ドル円相場への影響を整理すると次のようになる。 
① 民主党が上下両院を制する場合：予算や減税、関税政策に対する議会のチェックが強まり、
財政拡張への期待が後退することで、米長期金利の低下とドル安につながりやすい。FRBの人事に
対する議会の影響力が強まり、金融政策の独立性への懸念が和らぐことも、長期金利の押し下げ
要因となろう。一方で、予算案を巡る対立が先鋭化すれば、政府機関の閉鎖などを通じたリスク
回避の円買いが生じる可能性もある。いずれにしても、ドル円には下押し圧力がかかる公算が大
きい。 
② 下院は民主党、上院は共和党の"ねじれ"となる場合：現在の政策の大枠が維持される一方
で、新たな大型財政出動は難しくなる。相場の反応は限定的で、関心は再び日米の金融政策へと
移ることになろう。 
③ 共和党が両院の過半数を維持する場合：減税や規制緩和など拡張的な政策への期待から、米
長期金利の上昇とドル高が進みやすい。ドル円が再び160 円台を試し、介入への警戒が一気に高
まる展開も想定される。 
世論調査どおりであれば①ないし②の公算が大きく、ドル円にはどちらかと言えば円高寄りの
材料となろう。ただし、重要なのは選挙後の財政・通商政策や金利見通しの変化であり、選挙直
後の値動きだけで方向性を判断するのは早計だろう。

### Page 5

5/6 
4.  イラン情勢：選挙後の"重し"が外れる 
もう一つの大きな不透明要因がイラン情勢である。2 月 28 日に始まった米国・イスラエルとイ
ランの戦闘は、ホルムズ海峡の事実上の封鎖と、米国によるイラン港湾の海上封鎖を伴ったまま
長期化している。北海ブレント原油先物は20 日に一時1 バレル＝111.99 ドルと約5 ヶ月ぶりの高
値をつけた。 
原油高は、いくつかの経路でドル高円安に作用している。米国では、ガソリン価格の前年比
34％の上昇などを背景に、9 月の消費者物価が同＋3.7％へと加速し、FRBの追加利上げ観測を支え
ている。日本にとっては、エネルギー輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支が悪化し、実需の円売りが
増える。加えて、地政学リスクの高まりは"有事のドル買い"も誘いやすい。 
注目されるのは、トランプ大統領が「中間選挙前にはイランを攻撃しない」と明言している点
だ。言い換えれば、選挙後はこの制約が外れることになる。選挙後に軍事行動が再開されれば、
原油価格の一段高と米利上げ観測の強まりを通じて、ドル高円安が進むリスクがある。逆に、仲
介国を通じた交渉がまとまり、海峡の再開に向かえば、原油価格の下落とともに米長期金利が低
下し、ドル安円高へと振れやすい。実際、20～21 日には原油安を受けて米長期金利が低下し、ド
ルが売られる場面が見られた。

5.  日米の金融政策：12 月の"同時利上げ"の先が焦点 
FRBは9 月に約3 年ぶりの利上げに踏み切り、年内にもう1 回の利上げを示唆した。10 月14 日
に9 月の消費者物価が前年比3.7％と予想を上回ると、27～28 日のFOMCでの利上げ確率は一時5
割前後まで上昇した。しかし、17 日にウォーシュ議長が10 月会合の判断を予断しない姿勢を示し
たことで、利上げ確率は1 割台半ばまで低下している。市場では「10 月は見送り、12 月に利上
げ」との見方が大勢となった。一部には、12 月が今回の利上げ局面で最後になるとの予想も出て
いる。 
一方、日銀の植田総裁は、9 月の利上げ後に「政策の局面は変化した」と述べ、その後も基調的
な物価上昇率が2％に「かなり近づいている」との認識を示した。そのうえで、 「これ以上上がる
ことは容認できない」とも踏み込んでいる。29～30 日の決定会合では政策金利が据え置かれる公
算が大きいが、基調物価が2％に到達したと判断されると報じられている。 
日米がともに12 月に利上げすれば、名目の政策金利差は変わらない。為替にとって重要なの
は、その先の方向性の違いである。FF金利が明確に中立水準を超えるFRBの利上げが最終局面に近
づく一方、日銀は中立金利を目指して利上げを続ける。この構図が鮮明になれば、日米の実質金
利差は徐々に縮小に向かおう。逆に、原油高を受けて米国の物価上昇が加速し、FRBが一段の利上
げを迫られる展開となれば、実質金利差の縮小は見込めず、ドル円相場の膠着状態を演出しよ
う。

6.  今後の見通し：年末に向けて緩やかな円高を想定、ただし分岐は大きい 
以上を踏まえると、当面のドル円相場は、日米の金融政策会合と中間選挙という大きなイベン

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
トを控え、当面は現状の狭いレンジでの推移が続くと考えられる。10 月27～28 日のFOMCは据え置
きが市場の大勢だ。FOMCで利上げが見送られ、その後の日銀会合で基調物価2％到達が確認されて
12 月利上げへの地ならしが進めば、円高方向に振れやすい組み合わせとなる。しかし、市場では
FRBも年内利上げを実施するとの見方が強く、ドル円相場を動かす材料とはなりにくい。逆に、
FRBが10 月のFOMCで利上げを決定した場合、ドル円相場は159 円を超える円安進展の可能性が高
まる。 
年末にかけては、①中間選挙で民主党が議会の主導権を握り、②イラン情勢が交渉による解決
に向かい原油価格が落ち着き、③日銀が12 月に利上げを実施して来年以降も利上げ継続の姿勢を
示す、という三つの条件がそろえば、日米の実質金利差は縮小に向かう。その場合は、150 円台半
ばから前半への円高が進む可能性がある。投機筋の売建玉が再び積み上がっている局面であれ
ば、その巻き戻しが円高の勢いを増幅することも考えられる。 
一方で、①共和党の善戦、②選挙後のイランへの軍事行動の再開、③FRBの利上げ継続＋日銀の
利上げ慎重姿勢打ち出し、といった組み合わせとなれば、ドル円は再び160 円台を試すことにな
ろう。その場合、政府・日銀は過去の介入水準である160 円台前半から164 円近辺を意識しつ
つ、円安のスピードを抑えるための介入に踏み切る公算が大きい。ただし、これまでの経験が示
すように、ファンダメンタルズが変わらない中での介入は"時間を買う"政策にすぎず、効果が一
時的にとどまる可能性には注意が必要だ。 
筆者は、①米中間選挙は最低でも上下院のねじれ、場合によっては上院においても民主党の過
半数獲得、②選挙後に米国がイランへの軍事行動を再開、同時に仲介国を介した交渉を再開する
ものの、膠着状態が続く、③日米とも利上げ継続、をメインシナリオとしている。 
さらにリスク要因として、①米中間選挙の結果がなかなか確定せず、議会勢力の確定に時間が
かかる、②イラン情勢はフーシ派から攻撃を受けているサウジアラビアや、総選挙を終えたイス
ラエルによる本格的な攻撃参戦、の可能性を挙げる。 
このように不透明要因が多いことも足元のドル円相場の膠着の要因になっていると考えられる
が、短期的な値動きに一喜一憂するよりも、実質金利差という基本的な決定要因がどちらに動い
ているかを冷静に見極めることが重要であり、日米中央銀行の金融政策姿勢が最も注目される。

以 上

## Document 021: 2026-10-14_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ_5c83f50b

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-14_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ_5c83f50b.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ 
～物価上振れリスクが強まれば、一段の利上げ加速も～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
池田 亮平 
エコノミスト 阿部 大樹 
シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山 祐資 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 日銀は物価上振れ抑制に舵。中東情勢に起因する物価押し上げリスクは予断を許さず 
9 月日銀会合後の会見で植田総裁は「政策局面が変化した」と述べた（図表 1） 。これまで日銀は、
基調的物価の押し上げを金融政策運営の重点としてきたが、足元では 2％前後の物価上昇が定着しつ
つあるとみられる。こうしたもとで、金融政策運営の重点は物価上振れの回避に移っており、そのた
めに緩和的な金融環境をなるべく早く脱することが重視される。日銀の基本的なスタンスが、連続利
上げや、0.25％の利上げ幅を上回る大幅利上げは回避するのが望ましいというものであることも踏ま
えると、今後の利上げペースは 3 カ月に一回程度、0.25％ずつが基本になるだろう 1。 
ただし、金融政策の重点が物価上振れ回避にシフトしているなかでは、よりフォワードルッキング
な政策運営が求められる 2。したがって、 原油調達コストの上昇や円安など、 物価上振れリスクの強ま
り次第で利上げペースが速まる可能性がある。先行きの物価を押し上げる主なリスク要因として、日
図表1 9月会合後の総裁会見のポイント 図表2 WTI原油先物

### Page 2

2

銀は、原油高等の中東情勢影響、AI 関連需要の強さ、円安の三点を挙げている。このうち、原油価格
は足元で再び上昇しており、先行きの不確実性も大きい。中東情勢は、物価を取り巻く現下最大のリ
スクだろう。 
■ 不安定な情勢下で再び原油価格が上昇。タンカー運賃も急騰しダブルパンチの様相 
WTI原油先物は、9月に入って一時1バレル=100ドル台と、5月中旬以来の水準に上昇した （図表2） 。
足元では原油価格は幾分低下しているものの、水準としては依然高い状態が続いている。背景には、
中東産原油の供給懸念が強まったことがある。7月下旬以降、 サウジアラビアとイエメン・フーシ派に
よる戦闘が開始し、 ホルムズ海峡を代替する重要な原油輸送ルートとして活用されてきたバブ ・ エル ・
マンデブ海峡の通航リスクが高まった。 また、9月に入り、 サウジアラビアの原油パイプラインが攻撃
を受けて停止したと伝わると、中東産原油の域外輸送が一段と困難になるとの見方が広がった。 
中東情勢が再度悪化するなかで上昇しているのは原油価格だけではない。原油価格の上昇を大幅に
上回る勢いで、原油タンカーの運賃が急騰している（図表3）。8月以降に上昇基調が鮮明となり、9月
中旬には一時、中東情勢悪化前（2026年初）の約46倍、情勢悪化後の3～7月頃と比べても約3.2倍に達
した。 海峡航行リスクが高まるもと、 積み替えや迂回によって原油輸送の効率が悪化しているためだ。
海事データを活用して商品市場を分析しているベルギーの調査会社Kplerによると、 ホルムズ海峡の内
側にあるペルシャ湾から原油を運び出す船と、海峡の外側から最終目的地まで運ぶ船を分け、洋上で
原油を積み替える方式が広がっているようだ。 長距離輸送を担う船が危険な航路を通らずに済む反面、
積み替え作業や待機に時間を要するため、船を次の輸送に回しにくくなる。また、危険な海域を避け
るために遠回りする輸送も増え、船が一度の航海に費やす日数も長くなっている。より多くの輸送資
源が必要となったことで船舶需給がひっ迫し、タンカー運賃が大きく押し上げられている格好だ。

図表3 原油タンカーの運賃 図表4 原油輸入単価と原油価格

当たり換算収益。日本を含む東アジア向け運賃の代理指標。

前月比の伸びとして試算。中東情勢悪化を背景に、3月はドバイ原油
の価格形成機能が損なわれたと指摘されているため。原油輸入単価

### Page 3

3

■ 今後は日本の原油調達コストが上昇。企業間取引価格は反転加速を予想 
原油価格の上昇に原油タンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの日本の原油輸入単価（単位量あたり
の原油調達コスト）は一段と上昇する見込みだ3。 中東産原油が日本に輸送されるまでには1～2カ月程
度の期間を要するため、8月中旬以降の原油価格上昇 ・原油タンカー運賃上昇は10月以降の原油輸入単
価を押し上げ、その後国内の企業間取引価格へと影響が波及していくと考えられる。足元の原油価格
とタンカー運賃を踏まえると、10月の原油輸入単価は1バレル＝約140ドル程度と、中東情勢悪化後で
最も高い水準に上昇する可能性が高い（図表4）。この影響を輸入全体の平均価格（円建てベース）で
みると、10月の伸びは前年比＋29％程度と、中東情勢悪化後に最も輸入物価の伸びが加速した6・7月
に迫る勢いになる計算だ。さらに、その後は、石油化学製品などの関連財を中心に、国内の企業間取
引価格が上昇しよう。 足元で上昇圧力が一服しつつある国内企業物価は再び加速し、11月頃に同＋7％
台の伸びになると予想する（図表5）。 
加えて、原油輸入金額が膨らんで貿易収支が悪化すれば、円安圧力が強まる可能性がある。原油の
代替調達が進展した6月以降は1兆円に迫る貿易赤字が続いているが、特に10月以降は原油輸入単価が
上昇するもとで本邦貿易赤字が拡大する公算が大きく、実需面からの円安圧力が強まりやすい（図表
6） 。 足元の為替相場は、 米国の利上げ観測を背景に円安に振れやすい地合いだ。 そこに実需の動きが
重なり、1ドル＝160円を上回るようなドル高円安が進行すれば、輸入物価の上振れを通じて、国内企
業物価の伸びが一層高まるリスクがある。 
■ 物価上昇圧力が長期化する場合は利上げペース加速も視野に 
以上のように、当面の企業間取引価格に対しては押し上げ圧力が持続する公算が大きい。物価上昇
圧力が長期化するほど消費者物価への価格転嫁姿勢も強まる可能性があるだけに、日銀は物価上振れ
に対する高い警戒レベルを維持することになろう。 
仮に物価上振れリスクが大きく高まる場合には、 連続利上げ ・大幅利上げの可能性も排除できない。
中東情勢の混乱が長期化し、27年以降も原油輸入単価が一段と上振れることが見込まれる場合、ある

### Page 4

4

いは1ドル=160円を持続的に上回る水準まで円安が進展する場合には 、連続利上げや大幅利上げのシ
ナリオが現実味を帯びてくるだろう。

1 中立金利の水準は不透明であり、利上げの影響はタイムラグをともなって現れる。連続利上げ・大幅利上げによって意図せず
金融引き締めに転じれば、経済へのマイナス影響が必要以上に強まるリスクがある。 
2 中東情勢悪化以降で最初の利上げを決定した 6 月会合を振り返っても、日銀は川上の企業物価の加速を理由の一つとして利上
げに踏み切っている。6 月会合の声明文において日銀は、国内企業物価上昇率が前年比＋6.3％（5 月時点の速報ベース）に加速
したことを踏まえ、 「企業間取引におけるやや速いスピードでの価格転嫁が、消費者段階における幅広い品目の価格上昇に波及
していく可能性がある」と評価した。 
3 中東情勢悪化以降、日本の原油輸入単価は国際市況から大きく上振れている。背景として、主な代替調達先である米国産原油
が需給ひっ迫の懸念等から値上がりし、割高になったことなどが挙げられる。経済産業省発表によると、秋口以降は中東産原油
の調達が増加し、割高な米国産原油に対する依存度は低下すると見込まれるものの、本文記載のように中東産原油の輸送コスト
が大幅に高まっていることから、先行きも割高感の解消は見込みづらい。

至 50%；技術面上，次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年第一季進行

⚫ 公司基本資料 南亞科(2408.TT)公司簡介 
南亞科技(2408.TT)成立於 1995 年，隸屬於台塑集團，為全球領先的動
態隨機存取記憶體(DRAM)製造大廠 。 公司生產基地主要位於台灣 ， 擁有
多樣化的產品組合，涵蓋 DDR5、LPDDR5/5X、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X、
DDR3 及 LPDDR3 等產品線 ， 產品種類達數十顆 ， 服務全球數百家客戶 。
面對傳統記憶體產業之劇烈循環波動，公司近年積極推動技術升級與結
構性轉型，除中長期積極投入 1B(16 奈米)與 1C(12 奈米)先進製程轉換
及新五廠建置，並深入布局企業級 SSD(eSSD)、SmartNIC、BMC 等
AI 伺服器基礎設施，同時與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)利
基產品，正逐步由傳統大宗商品化DRAM 廠，轉型為具備高附加價值與
高敏捷度的全方位利基型記憶體解決方案供應商 。

3Q26 獲利大幅跳升，低價長約重簽驅動 ASP 季增逾六成

第三季集中到期並重新議價(對 ASP 季增貢獻度超過半數)。在 ASP 大
幅 上 漲 且 生 產 成 本 結 構 穩 定 的 推 升 下 ， 單 季 毛 利 率 跳 升 至  
85.7%(Q+6.2ppt / Y+67.2ppt) 。費用方面，因增發員工薪酬獎勵，研發

為 75.88 億元 。 綜合以上 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 3Q26 營業收入為 1,336.49
億元， Q+61.90%/Y+611.70% 、 毛 利 率  85.70% ，
Q+6.21ppt/Y+67.25ppt、 營業利益率 79.98%，Q+6.30ppt/Y+74.02ppt、
稅後純益 876.27 億元，Q+74.58%/Y+虧轉盈、EPS 為 25.40 元。

2026 年受惠 DRAM 報價持續上漲與 AI 伺服器需求，獲利創歷史新高 
展望 2026 年全年，南亞科(2408.TT) 前三季累計淨利已高達 1,639 億
元，累計EPS 達 49.28 元，公司維持 2026 全年位元銷售量成長高十
位數百分比的目標；合約與定價機制上，公司目前長約(LTA)覆蓋率落在 
50% 至 70% 之間，其中 3 年期以上長約占總出貨量 20% 以上，履
約率高達 100%，預期第四季 DRAM 供需緊缺狀態延續，合約平均售
價保持溫和成長；需求端方面，GPU、TPU、CPU 及 ASIC 等 AI 基
礎建設建置強勁，推升 AI 伺服器與基礎設施相關產品之營收占比突破 
20%； 產品組合中 DDR4 與 LPDDR4 合計營收占比超過 60%，DDR5 
占比約 10%。管理層指引目前產業短期內難以緩解供給壓力，隨著第四
季 DRAM ASP 持續看漲 ， 公司毛利率表現有機會再進一步拉升 。 綜合以
上，研究部預估南亞科(2408.TT) 2026 年營業收入為 4,190.95 億元，
Y+529.40%、毛利率  82.61%，Y+60.10ppt、營業利益率  76.83%，
Y+68.96ppt、稅後純益 2,642.16 億元，Y+3,894.93%、EPS 為 76.58
元。

3 
長鑫存儲(CXMT)技術進展與產業影響 
長鑫存儲(CXMT)於 2026 年 9 月宣佈第五代 DRAM 制程(G5)進入量產階
段。儘管目前 G5 產品仍處於客戶驗證與良率提升階段，距離大規模商
業化出貨尚需時間 ， 但已顯示中國DRAM 技術發展速度明顯超出市場原
先預期。 長鑫 HBM3E 良率已達 40%左右。市場普遍認為，長鑫在無法
取得 EUV 設備的情況下，主要透過 ArF DUV 浸潤式曝光搭配多重曝光
(Multiple Patterning) 技術推進制程微縮，因此雖然能夠達到接近國際大
廠先進 DRAM 節點的設計能力 ， 但製造成本 、 良率與生產效率仍與三星 、
SK 海力士及美光存在差距。未來能否持續提升至國際大廠 70%以上的
量產水準，將是觀察長鑫HBM 競爭力的重要指標。短期而言，長鑫技術
進步對台廠商影響有限，長鑫目前仍面臨良率提升與客戶驗證所需時
間，短期內難以快速增加有效供給，因此全球 DRAM 供應緊張的局面仍
將延續至 2027 至 2028 年。相較之下，三星、SK 海力士與美光持續將
產能轉向 HBM 產品，反而造成 DDR4 供給逐漸退出市場，進一步支撐
DDR4 價格維持強勢，有利於以 DDR4 產品為主的南亞科營運表現；然
而，中長期則需注意，長鑫 DRAM 投片量將於 2027 年底達到約 30K 
WPM，並持續擴產，若未來 G5 良率持續改善、HBM 技術逐步成熟，
加上中國市場本土化政策支持，長鑫未來在中低階 DDR5、PC DRAM
及中國本土市場的競爭力可能快速提升 ， 進而對全球DRAM 供需格局產
生實質影響。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)4F²技術 
長鑫存儲在 10 月 12 日有表示年底推出 4F²架構 DDR5 伺服器記憶體，
4F²是將每個記憶體儲存單元配置在面積相當於 4 倍 F 平方的區域內 ， 其
中 F 代表半導體制程的最小線寬，這種設計能讓同一顆晶片容納更多儲
存單元，進而提高記憶體容量。目前，主流商用 DRAM 仍採用 6F 平方
儲存單元結構。長鑫存儲表示，其4F 平方設計的核心，在於自主研發的
垂直環繞閘極(Vertical Surrounding Gate，VSG)電晶體，這項技術透過
垂直閘極架構改善漏電問題，同時大幅縮小單元面積，提高儲存密度。
SK 海力士(SK Hynix) 已於 2025 年公佈 4F 平方技術發展藍圖，另外，
根據南韓媒體今年 4 月的報導，三星電子(Samsung Electronics)計畫於
2028 年前，量產採用這項新設計、制程低於 10 奈米的 DRAM。

結論與建議 
展望未來 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存 ，2027 年上半年位元出
貨量預期將保持平穩 ， 待4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後 ， 出貨量將恢復逐步
成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開始進場裝機，第一

力，依據美光27 年本益比區間約 5 至 6.6 倍，研究部給予5.5 倍本益比
評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

### Page 3

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Rather than now slowing down, it’s actually getting stronger
TSMC reported an upbeat 3Q26 result with robust 4Q26 outlook. It guided for
4Q26 revenue of US$52.3–54.3 billion, representing about 14% QoQ growth and
58% year-over-year growth at the midpoint (based on FX at NT$31.8 per USD).
4Q26 GM/OPM is expected at 65–67% and 56.5–58.5%. TSMC reiterated its 2026
capex to be US$60–64 billion and said investment from 2026 to 2028 will be

significantly higher than in the previous three years, reflecting strong multi-year
customer demand, including agentic AI. It is also worth noting that TSMC plans to
begin using the High-NA EUV technology around 2030 and develop a 12-inch
mask capability by 2031, when it expects the tools to offer sufficient maturity and
cost effectiveness. Management said this timing does not undermine its
confidence in technology leadership.
Management described AI-related demand as exceptionally strong, supported by

accelerators, networking chips, and rising demand for CPUs associated with
agentic AI. TSMC’s previously guided AI GPU/accelerator CAGR to be mid-to-high
50%, but now sees more upside ahead. Given rising concern on AI expanding
peaking out, the management said it is clear that its capacity planning strategy has
considered customer forecasts, data-center construction, power availability, and
other supply-chain constraints. Management still judges demand to be above

available supply and is working to close the gap, while emphasizing disciplined
planning to limit the risk of overbuilding.
Figure 1. TSMC – 3Q26 results comparison
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Capacity expansion plan and allocation
During the earnings call, TSMC also mentioned that despite the tight capacity
supply, customers cannot generally sublet or auction wafer capacity, while there is

limited case that collaboration is through mask-license agreements. For the
advanced packaging, TSMC has announced its Global Foundries and VIS’
agreement on interposer as part of its backend supply strategy, intended to help
ensure customers have enough capacity to bring products to market; It also
reiterated that TSMC’s CoWoS remains as the leading customer’s choice and is
already in volume production at 5x reticle size, while it is developing 9x and larger

solutions and evaluating CoPoS.

Reiterate Buy, best position in AI arena
Our earnings projection is largely unchanged for 2027 /2028 earnings. While its
capex expansion, overseas operation and N2 will likely be a margin dilution factor
initially, due to its cross-node leverage, high UTR and yield rate, we believe its GM
downside is manageable. Overall, we see strong advanced node demand into next
year and the long-term order visibility will be supported for higher capex cycle.  We

maintain our TP to NT$4,000, based on unchanged 25x of 2027 EPS estimate;
reiterate Buy.

n4Q26 guidance
–Sales: US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the midpoint)

than 40% growth to +44% growth
–Full year capex: retain its 2026 capex guidance at US$60-64bn (70-80%
advanced process, 10% specialty, 10-20% for advanced packaging, testing

and masks)
–GM outlook
•4Q GM to be impacted by N2 and overseas fab ramps, but partially
supported by productivity improvement, UTR enhancement and
optimizing capacity allocation

2026; with more new capacity expansion in the US, the margin dilution
impact would widen to 3-4ppts in the next few years.
–Long-term outlook
•Retains 5-year AI sales CAGR (2024-2029) remains at high-50% or above

nCapacity planning
–TSMC currently has 20 fabs in total. It plans to add 13 fabs in Taiwan and add
5-6 overseas fabs.
–N2 capacity will double in 2027; it has also started preparing for A14 capacity
with volume production starting from 2028.
–US Arizona fab 2 construction is complete and begin volume production in
3Q27, ahead of schedule.
–JP fab 2 would produce N3 with mass production from 2028

### Page 10

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
10
TSMC
Company description
TSMC is the founder and leader of the dedicated IC foundry segment. The
company has built its reputation by offering advanced and "more-than-
Moore" wafer production processes and industry-leading manufacturing
efficiency. From its inception in 1987, TSMC has consistently offered the
foundry segment's leading technologies and TSMC-compatible design
services.
Investment strategy

We rate TSMC shares as Buy. We expect TSMC to deliver sales growth of
>40% in recent years thanks to strong growth from HPC/AI as well as stable
demand growth from smartphone, Io T, and automotive on tech migration and
a shortened replacment cycle. CPU outsourcing to TSMC offers further
growth potential. A rising cash dividend should also support the share price.
While TSMC has been increasing its capex meaningfully since 2009, its cost
structure remaisn competitive through continuous product mix

improvements, efficiency enhancements, and cost reductions.
Valuation
Our target price for TSMC shares of NT$4,000 is based on 25x our 2027E
EPS, higher than its three-year average forward PER, versus global peers’
average of 16-25x. We believe our target PER is justified by TSMC's leading
position in advanced process node and robust AI demand growth outlook.
Given its continuing leadership in the semiconductor foundry industry, we
believe TSMC’s stable order visibility and earnings outlook make it less

vulnerable to a potential global economic downturn than some of its peers.
Our target price equates to 2026E/27E P/E of ~ 36x/25x and 2026E/27E P/B of
~13x/10x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from achieving our target
price include: 1) weakness in the global semiconductor market; 2) larger-
than-expected margin contractions due to depreciation cost increases and
strong NTD; 3) competitors entering the foundry business; 4) longer-than-

expected digestion of inventory in the supply chain; and 5) a slowdown in
demand either due to a global economic downturn or a global trade
disruption triggered by tariffs.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 120: 261015_ms_TSMC

### Page 1

M
Idea
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
No slowdown of AI semi
demand; reiterate OW
TSMC is further expanding capacity after considering other
constraints, such as data center build, power and other
components supply.  We think the risk of over-capacity is small,
and stay OW.
TSMC sees robust AI semi demand: Despite CSP power shortages and rising
financing costs, TSMC sees demand far above its capacity. It sees no signs of a
slowdown, which may ease some market concerns. The company did not update its

AI semi CAGR guidance (55-60%), saying it is not meaningful as  the demand is
expanding rapidly.
Comments on  wafer foundry partnership (TeraFab) and competition (Intel): For
overseas fabs, TSMC is evaluating a TeraFab partnership, but TSMC must run the
partner fab. TSMC is also considering other fab campus locations, including Texas.
We asked management about IDM outsourcing from Intel and implications for A14
capacity demand. Management said it would try to be fair to all customers. On

competition, TSMC would expand capacity and “leave no food on the table” for
competitors.
On advanced packaging, we asked whether TSMC would help customers using
Intel EMIB-T advanced packaging: The company is confident about 9x and 14x
reticle size chip support. TSMC’s principle is to help customers ramp products
smoothly, so we take it as no objection if Google/MediaTek needs to adopt CoWoS/
CoPoS in future TPU versions, as we articulated in MediaTek: TSMC CoWoS support

eases MediaTek's 2028 TPU production concerns
We raise 2027/28 capex estimates to factor in 2nm and 1.4nm expansion: The
company maintained its 2026 capex guidance (US$60-64bn) and didn’t provide
next-year capex guidance. That said, it  mentioned more than a doubling of 2nm
capacity build by 2027.  Our initial cross checks with equipment venders suggest
EUV tool bookings double in 2027 (vs. 2026), followed by 25-30% growth in 2028.
We therefore raise our 2027/28 capex assumption to US$83/92bn.

3Q26 results review – margin miss, but revenue upside: Pease see Exhibit 1  and
Exhibit 2  for our earnings review. We lower our 2026 and 2027 margin
assumptions,  offset by higher revenue. We maintain our price target at NT$3,088
and reiterate our OW rating.

TSMC 3Q26 earnings and 4Q26 guidance  review
3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance review – margin miss, but revenue upside: 3Q26
margin of 66.4% misses our forecast of 67.5%, but was largely in-line with consensus,
given the margin dilution of 2nm node and overseas operation dilution.
The 4Q26 margin midpoint of 66% shows continued pressure. TSMC guides for 4Q26
revenue to grow 14% Q/Q at the midpoint on very strong demand, in line with our 10-15%

Q/Q expectation. Full-year revenue guidance is raised to 44% Y/Y, also similar to our
preview.

TSMC: Earnings estimate revisions
Our earnings forecasts decline 3% for 2026,  3% for  2027 and is largely unchanged  for
2028: We lower our 2026/27 earnings forecast mostly due to the margin miss of 3Q26
results and weaker than our initial expectation of 4Q26 margin guidance, and also the
wider overseas fab margin dilution into 2027. We slightly raise the revenue assumption for
2027/2028 as TSMC suggested it would speed up the 3nm/2nm capacity build, but that

also comes with larger depreciation and margin pressure when new production ramps.

Our PT is unchanged at NT$3,088. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027

EPS forecast. TSMC currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of
16.5x since 2018. We find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an
intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.

Our bull and bear case scenario values remain at NT$3,720 and NT$1,700, respectively.

NT$3,720.00
24x 2027e EPS
TSMC dominates foundry services: 1)
Breakthrough in EUV tech and materials
accelerates node migration to 3nm and
beyond. 2) Intel outsources its server CPU
production to TSMC sooner than expected.
3) Intel or Samsung exits leading-edge
foundry business. 4) New tech megatrends
such as 6G or AI drive global semi revenue

growth and increase demand for leading
edge nodes. 5) Cost per transistor decreases
further along with node migration.
NT$3,088.00
20x 2027e EPS
Leading position in leading-edge logic
foundry services with some gross margin
expansion: 1) Global semi revenue CAGR is
sustained in the high single digits in 2025-
28. 2) TSMC continues to lead in market
share in 16nm, 7nm, 5nm, 3nm, and 2nm. 3)
It continues to observe robust AI-related
demand. 4) Cost per transistor does not

decrease meaningfully in 3nm and 2nm,
while capex per k is more demanding than
for 7nm and 5nm.
NT$1,700.00
11x 2027e EPS
TSMC's dominance in foundry business
erodes: 1) Global ex-memory semi revenue
growth is weaker than expected in 2025-28.
2) Declining trailing-edge market share
causes revenue shortfall. 3) Intel's
contribution is weaker than expected. Intel
and/or Samsung successfully develop
leading-edge process technology. 4) Intel's
foundry business execution proves

successful and weighs on TSMC with order
competition. 5) Demand for 2nm is less than
expected given high transistor cost for
foundry customers.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 126: 261016_ms_cotech

### Page 1

M
Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Announces additional
investment in Malaysia
subsidiary
What’s new? Co-Tech’s board today approved an additional NT$1.4bn investment in
its 100%-owned subsidiary, CoTech Development (Penang) Sdn. Bhd., to be financed
with internal funds or bank loans. This follows the company’s September 14
announcement that it would establish a subsidiary in Southeast Asia (note).
Our view: Our latest checks suggest that, due to strong demand from key

customers, the Malaysia plant will primarily produce advanced HVLP copper foil
rather than the RG-series foil originally planned. The new plant is also likely to be
larger than Yunlin Plant III, where Co-Tech plans to add ~600 tons of HVLP3/4
capacity by end-2027. Volume production in Malaysia is unlikely to start before late
2028, so most of the ramp-up should fall in 2029, contributing to that year’s
earnings. On funding, our checks also suggest Co-Tech will finance further

investment in the new plant’s construction and equipment by issuing convertible
bonds.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13 Sep
2026)

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Co-Tech Development Corp. (8358.TWO)
Base case, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity
of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1%), medium-term growth
rate of 13%, and terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger AI demand
n Slower-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors
n Faster-than-expected material upgrades
n Slower-than-expected CPO adoption

Risks to Downside
n Weaker AI demand
n Faster-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Gigabyte (2376.TW): AI servers to drive 2H26 growth; Capex 
commitments remain strong; Neutral
20 October 2026 | 10:16PM CST
3-month revenues preview: We expect sequential revenue growth in Oct-Dec on a 
YoY basis, driven by rising AI server racks, leading our 4Q26 revenues YoY to 
accelerate to 79% (vs. 60% / 41% YoY in 1Q26 / 2Q26). However, we expect revenue 
decline in Oct-Dec on a MoM basis which we attribute to AI servers model transition 
and the high base in Sep; nevertheless, we continue to expect revenues sequentially 
increase qoq in 4Q26E. We expect the stronger growth in 2H26 to be driven by 
ongoing AI spending, customers’ faster AI deployment, and Gigabyte’s expanding 
customer base and enhancing backlog visibility. Customers’ infrastructure readiness 
and components procurement should continue to improve in 2H26, supporting the 
procurement on AI servers. The company maintains strong capex commitment, and 
we expect 2H26 capex to stay at a similar high level vs. 1H26, leading 2026 capex to 
grow at 148% YoY to NT$2.9bn to capture the strong AI servers demand, and 
customers’ requirements on diversifying production sites (e.g., Gigabyte is across 
Taiwan, US, Europe, and Southeast Asia).

Earnings revision: We factor in Gigabyte’s Sep revenue and raise our 2026E net

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Gigabyte 
We are Neutral rated on Gigabyte with a 12-month target price of NT$492. Our TP is 
based on a 14.2x 2027E P/E which is based on PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E 
multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s share 
performance. 
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery, 2) faster-/slower-than-expected gaming PC growth, and 3) 
faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 20 Oct 2026 close.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀 
 2Q26 毛利率 25.87%，低於預期，主要仍是中國毛利率拖累，2Q26 
EPS 2.02 元:迅得 2Q26 EPS 2.02 元，與元富預估 2 元差異不大。
2Q26 營收 18.24 億元， +5%QOQ，+12%YOY，2Q26 毛利 率
25.87%，低於元富預估的 26.5%，主要是中國地區仍有低價競爭影
響，本業獲利達成率 99%，業外收益挹注下，2Q26 EPS 2.02 元。 
 交期拉長，2027 年預計擴產 40%，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元:載板
客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極
布局轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，由於載板面積增大， 技術難度提
升，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。晶圓製造及
OSAT 隨客戶台灣及海外積極擴產， 交期過去 4-6 個月現在已經拉長
到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元， 預計2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 220 元：3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長，新產
能逐步開出， 故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 
11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)，目標價 220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

迅得為自動化設備供應商 
 迅得為自動化設備供應商 
迅得機械成立於 1999 年，主要產品為印刷電路板、軟板、光電及半導體物流系
統之自動化解決方案，2025 年營收比重可區分為 PCB 事業部(41%)、半導體-
載板事業部 13%、半導體-晶圓事業部 12%、半導體-封裝事業部 32%，面板事
業部 2%。主要客戶包括欣興、南亞、景碩、台郡、臻鼎、日月光、台積電、友
達、群創等。

迅得 2Q26 EPS 2.02 元 ， 與元富預估2 元差異不大 ， 符合預期 。 本業來看 ，2Q26
營收 18.24 億元，+5%QOQ，+12%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 25.87%，低於元富預

迅得九月營收 6.95 億元，+14%MOM，+31%YOY，營收達成率 104%，優於
預期。累計 3Q26 營收 19.45 億元，+6.6%QOQ，+16.7%YOY，預估 3Q26 毛
利率 27%，預估 3Q26 EPS 2.26 元。展望第四季，原估 4Q26 營收 19.63 億元，
現因 3Q26 營收優於預期，故上修 4Q26 營收至 20.42 億元，維持 4Q26 營收
+5%QOQ 之預估，故預估 4Q26 營收 20.42 億元，+5%QOQ，+26.4%YOY，
毛利率 27.5%，預估 4Q26 EPS 2.47 元。

個股報告

載板 、 晶圓客戶 、OSAT 客戶均樂
觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元 
迅得 1H26 營收占比為 PCB 4 1%，載板 16%，晶圓製造 16%，封裝 25%，其
他 2%。PCB 主要產品以自動上下料、物件搬運及智慧倉儲為主； IC 載板亦為
自動上下料較多，整廠規畫約佔比 60%，單機搬運 20%，倉儲 15~20%；半導
體晶圓廠則以倉儲為主，台灣部分標準倉儲較多，微型倉儲較少， 海外部分目
前未出貨標準倉儲，僅供應微型倉儲，主要是安裝時間及人力配置考量；封裝
OSAT 廠主要供應倉儲佔比 60~70%，自動化搬運約 30%。

載板客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極布局
轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，以欣興資本支出來看，2026 年 340 億元，2027
年長交期採購設備訂單金額 92 億元 ， 均將投入桃園楊梅廠及光復廠 ； 景碩2026
年資本支出 140 億元(原本是 80 億元)，將投入於六廠建置及設備導入，2027
年資本支出為 160 億元(原本是 100 億元)， 擴產進度明確 。 由於載板面積增大，
技術難度提升 ，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出 ，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。

晶圓製造部分，隨 客戶台灣及海外積極擴產，因目前現有自動化廠商均無單一
廠商可獨立供足客戶所需 自動化設備量體，故每個案件 公司均有相對應配比，
以海外廠來說，考量人力成本及安裝時間公司僅提供微型倉儲為主。 OSAT 部
分，客戶拉貨持續積極，半導體業務維持樂觀。以交期來看，過去 4~6 個月現
在已經拉長到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元，預計 2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。

維持買進，目標價 220 元

維持買進評等，目標價 220 元 
3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長 ， 新產能逐步開出 ， 故維持買進 ， 預估2026 年 EPS 
8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)， 目標價220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

## Document 196: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 229: AI崩了，法债爆了，急需美债“避险”！10年期美债收益率创“硅谷银行危机”来最大跌幅

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI崩了，法债爆了，急需美债“避险”！10年期美债收益率创“硅谷银行危机”来最大跌幅.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛分析师指出， AI 资本⽀出回撤意味着债券潜在供给减少，效果近似量化宽松。叠加法
国主权债务危机加速恶化与中东油价飙升，推动避险资⾦流⼊美债。 10 年期美债收益率单
⽇下⾏ 19.3 个基点重回 5% 以下，为 2023 年 3 ⽉ 10 ⽇硅⾕银⾏危机以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。
摘要：
AI 科技股的⼀轮集中抛售，意外成为美债市场三年来最猛烈的单⽇反弹的导⽕索。
周四， 10 年期美债收益率单⽇下⾏ 19.3 个基点⾄ 4.952% 重回 5% 以下，为 2023 年 3 ⽉ 10 ⽇硅⾕银
⾏危机以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。据 Dow Jones Market Data ，过去⼗年仅有 15 个交易⽇出现过如此幅
度的单⽇波动，涵盖 2020 年新冠危机、 2023 年区域银⾏危机、 2025 年 4 ⽉关税冲击以及今年 2 ⽉
美以对伊朗发动战争等重⼤事件。
2 年期收益率下⾏ 18.8 个基点⾄ 4.635% ，为 2025 年 8 ⽉ 1 ⽇以来最⼤单⽇跌幅； 30 年期收益率下
⾏ 15.1 个基点⾄ 5.286% ，为 2024 年 8 ⽉以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。 5 年期和 7 年期美债当天甚⾄下跌了

20 个基点。
PGIM Credit 全球债券主管 Robert Tipp 说： “中东局势升级、法国问题持续恶化以及 AI 领域疑虑
上升 —— 你看到的是多重压⼒同时爆发。”
他补充道： “正常交易⽇，⼀次像样的波动也就⼏个基点。今天你看到的是三倍于此。”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

三重压⼒同时引爆
Mackenzie Investments ⾸席固收策略师 Dustin Reid 说： “这种级别的波动，通常意味着某种恐
慌或危机正在发⽣。 ”
他的判断是： “我们正处于⼀个围绕 AI 过度扩张的主题之中。” 这触发了投资者开始 “ 落袋为安 ” ，资
⾦从股票轮转⾄美债和固收市场。
具体来看，三条线索同时触发：
其⼀， AI 财报季的 “ 不及预期 ” 。 Lam Research 业绩虽强但未突破隐性预期上限，指引⽆明显上
⾏空间；特斯拉将全年资本⽀出指引从逾 250 亿美元下调⾄约 200 亿美元； Meta 将全年资本⽀出
指引下限上调⾄ 1350 亿美元，但这意味着仅第四季度单季⽀出即需达到 490 亿⾄ 590 亿美元，市
场对执⾏可⾏性产⽣疑虑。 Meta 已与亚⻢逊⼀道进⼊⾃由现⾦流为负的阵营。
其⼆，法国主权债务危机加剧。 法国 10 年期国债收益率盘中⼀度升⾄ 5.142% ，为 2002 年 6 ⽉以
来最⾼盘中⽔平，收盘报 5.082% 。法国与德国 10 年期国债利差扩⼤⾄逾 170 个基点，创 2011 年

欧债危机以来最⾼；更值得注意的是，法国 10 年期收益率⼀度⾼于意⼤利同期限收益率约 50 个
基点 —— 历史上从未出现过这⼀情形。 法国 CDS 超过 100 个基点，为 2012 年以来⾸次。
其三，油价飙升叠加中东紧张局势。 胡塞武装对沙特发动新⼀轮袭击，沙特阿美沙伊巴赫油⽥
遭⽆⼈机袭击， WTI 原油价格逼近每桶 100 美元的伊朗战争⾼点。布伦特原油重上每桶 112 美
元。
DWS 美洲固收主管 George Catrambone 表⽰，尽管上⽉美债收益率⼤幅上⾏， “ 美债仍具备避险
资产属性 ” ，法国的动荡 “ 带来了⼀定的避险买盘 ” 。
AI 资本⽀出退潮，债市的 “ 另类宽松 ”
⾼盛 Rich Privorotsky 在给机构客⼾的晨报中将这⼀现象定性为 “ 令⼈瞩⽬的反⾝性 ” ：
逻辑链条清晰：超⼤规模云计算企业发⾏债务的规模已可与美国国债相提并论， AI 资本⽀出的任
何收缩，实质上等同于减少债券市场的潜在供给压⼒。
AI 疲软重新定价了资本⽀出周期，减轻了潜在债券发⾏的压⼒，并⽇益演变为⼀次增⻓冲击。反

⾝性之强令⼈瞩⽬ —— 正是制造股票问题的那件事，正在帮助解决利率问题。

### Page 3

Meta 的案例最具说明意义。巴克莱信⽤分析师 Andrew Keches 在财报后报告中写道：
换⾔之， Meta 现在既需要债市，也需要股市，⽽且两者都需要⼤量。
数据中⼼ CDS 利差重新飙升⾄历史新⾼；超⼤规模云计算企业整体信⽤⻛险持续攀升，并开始
向整个信⽤市场蔓延 —— 欧元区⾼收益信⽤违约互换指数 iTraxx Europe Crossover 升⾄ 329 个
基点，为近七个⽉最⾼⽔平。
⾼盛： CTA 空头回补 “ 空间还很⼤ ”
这轮美债涨势背后， CTA 空头回补是重要推⼿之⼀。⾼盛期货交易台数据显⽰，趋势跟踪量化
基⾦⽬前已是⾼盛所追踪的每个债券市场的净买家。
本周预计买⼊ 1800 万美元 DV01 ，若收益率持续下⾏可升⾄ 2700 万；⼀个⽉维度基准买⼊规模
将达 5500 万美元 DV01 ，但这仅约为其模拟净空仓规模的三分之⼀ —— 空头回补压⼒仍有相当⼤
的释放空间。
我们对此的热情，在⼀定程度上被 14C ⽂件中暗⽰的庞⼤⽀出野⼼所削弱。我们的判断是，

Meta 未来将同时依赖信贷市场和股票市场，⽽债券发⾏的绝对规模也可能上升。我们继续预计
近期将有债券发⾏，这将在⼀段时间内压制信⽤利差。

### Page 4

与此同时， CME 未平仓合约数据显⽰，⻓债期货此前积累了⼤规模新增空头头⼨，这批空头在
美债上涨过程中⾯临被动回补压⼒，进⼀步助推了涨势。
期权市场：押注收益率继续下⾏
据彭博报道，美债期权对冲成本周四飙升⾄ 3 ⽉以来最⾼⽔平。
⼀项衡量指标显⽰，市场参与者为⻓期美债看涨期权⽀付的溢价⾼于看跌期权，为七个⽉来⾸
次 —— 这意味着投资者正在为债券进⼀步上涨做准备。
当⽇最引⼈注⽬的头⼨之⼀：⼀名交易员斥资约 700 万美元期权费，在上午交易时段建⽴起约
6.5 万张合约的头⼨，押注 10 年期收益率在⼀个⽉内降⾄ 4.6% 附近。
股市： AI 资本⽀出叙事全⾯动摇
纳斯达克指数当⽇跌 2.6% ，费城半导体指数跌超 7% ，英特尔、 AMD 跌超 8% ，⾼通跌超 3% 。标
普 500 指数跌 1.5% ，但等权重标普 500 当⽇相对标普 500 录得 2024 年 7 ⽉以来最佳单⽇表现 ——
防御性板块医疗、公⽤事业逆势⾛强。
⾼盛 Prime Brokerage 数据显⽰，基本⾯多空策略当⽇下跌 1.1% ，系统化策略下跌 1.2% ，多策

略下跌 1.4%—— 这是⾃新冠疫情以来三类策略⾸次在同⼀天均跌超 1% 。对冲基⾦重仓多空组合
（ GSTHVIPP ）当⽇下跌约 4.2% ，为 2011 年以来最⼤单⽇跌幅，本⽉累计跌幅达 10.4% 。

### Page 5

FBB Capital Partners 研究总监 Michael Bailey 说：
⾼盛交易员将这⼀⾏情定性为 “ 解仓⾛势 ” ，流向数据显⽰ AI 主题 “ 零进攻迹象 ” 。
CFRA Research ⾸席投资策略师 Sam Stovall 说： “投资者现在很担⼼，在想：这是不是那场⼤
规模抛售的开始？” 他指出，科技股⽬前约占标普 500 市值的 40% 。
Tipp 则认为，这轮⾏情是 “ 获利了结，⽽⾮全⾯的衰退恐慌。 ”
市场读出的信号：增⻓威胁，⽽⾮通胀
分析认为，油价飙升之际，美债同步被抢购 —— 市场将⾼油价解读为增⻓威胁，⽽⾮通胀威
胁。这对⻛险资产⽽⾔，是⼀个⽐指数跌幅本⾝更令⼈担忧的信号。
加息预期也随之收敛。⽬前市场完全定价于明年 1 ⽉加息 25 个基点，⽽⾮此前预期的 12 ⽉。
Tipp 在 PGIM 表⽰，若这些压⼒打在⼀个没有定价美联储持续加息的市场上， “ 那还另当别论 ” 。
但现在市场已定价了数次加息， “你就有了⼀个市场将开始质疑美联储能否在未来⼏个季度继续

加息的局⾯。 ”
今天的市场给投资者提供了⼀个今年以来最为剧烈的轮动版本。 Meta 财报点燃了空头，引发了
抛售近期 AI 赢家、买⼊医疗、消费和公⽤事业等 ' ⽆聊即美 ' 板块的浪潮。

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 265: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Source: Bank of America internal data

### Page 2

  I NSTI TUTE

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories

With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 285: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

### Page 2

2 01 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.  
Exhibit 8: A large and growing proportion of net worth is held by 
older cohorts 
Distribution of household wealth by age ($, trillions)

Source: Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: Healthcare is a significant cost for older households 
Healthcare spending as a percentage of overall household expenditure by 
age groups (%)

Source: Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS)
